# CLAUDE.md

Guidance for Claude Code when working with this repository.

---

## Project Overview

**JARVIS AI-OS** - An AI-controlled operating system using seL4 microkernel with autonomous decision-making. AI runs at Ring 3 (50-500ms decisions), microkernel runs at Ring 0 (<1ms interrupts). 36-month timeline.

| Phase | Status | Period | Summary |
|-------|--------|--------|---------|
| Phase 0 | COMPLETE | Months 1-6 | Validation (80% success, GO decision) |
| Phase 1 | COMPLETE | Months 6-12 | PoC on x86 QEMU (26/26 weeks, Dec 2025) |
| Phase 2 | COMPLETE | Months 12-24 | Alpha on Pi 4 bare metal (21 drivers, 30-day stability) |
| **Phase 3** | **COMPLETE (beta)** | Months 24-36 | Beta on x86-64 bare metal (**LLM inference on seL4 VERIFIED**) — v0.2.1-beta tagged (30-day soak deferred) |
| Phase 4 | COMPLETE | Months 36+ | Production v1.0 — v1.0.0 SHIPPED 2026-06-26 (tag bdf0951, MIT, public) |
| Phase 5 | SUBSTANTIALLY COMPLETE | Months 36+ | Memory — **all 7 goals mechanism-proven (2026-07-04)**: Arc 1 (#1/#2/#3/#6) deployed default-ON; Arc 2 #5/#4 proven + gated-off (activate Phase 6); #7 folded into #4; `memory` tag proposed as `v1.1.0-memory` (plan: `phase5/docs/PHASE_5_PLAN.md`) |
| Phase 6 | IN PROGRESS | Months 36+ | Butler — plan (`phase6/docs/PHASE_6_PLAN.md`) + keystone doc (`PHASE_6_GOAL_K_IT_ACTS.md`, K = "it-acts" spine + live SHIELD). `TLM_F_PROACTIVE` latched at the first [STATS] window — early-boot honestly lacks it; `behaviors_fired`/`mask`/`last` HONEST-0 on the healthy sub-1h run; err=0 to q=83,100+, NN=6, coherent, 5.41 tok/s live; **the COMPLETE Phase-6 flag set co-live**: MEMORY+CONTEXT+RETRIEVAL+CACHE_GROWTH+ACTIONS+MONITORS+WAKE+PROACTIVE) + the live console card rendered from the real feed (Playwright vs the live SSE: total "0", 5 rows "quiet", simulated=false). **The deployed image now runs the FULL proactive butler: memory + the K self-heal action gate + always-on monitors + the event-driven wake lane + the ≥5 registry INFORM behaviors.** Next Phase-6 goals: 6-5 (control-IN) / 6-6 (routing ≥95%) / 6-7 (the 7-day supervised exit).** **Boot_id=17 side-finding 2026-07-11: FIRST on-wire NONZERO v8 validated** — a 28.7 h unattended run (q=28.0M, err=0, restart_count=0) crossed the 1 h + 24 h uptime marks → `monitors_fired=2`/`last_monitor_event=5` on the wire + the two JACT `action=2` uptime records, zero false positives (closes 6-1's one UNVERIFIED item).** **Goal 6-5 (control-IN / natural-language primary — the SECURITY LONG POLE) goal doc authored 2026-07-13 (`phase6/docs/PHASE_6_GOAL_6-5_CONTROL_IN.md`) — PLAN-FIRST, awaiting strategist review; NO code/flag/wire changes.** Turns the read-only console two-way (the box's FIRST untrusted inbound); a 6-item HARD checklist (fuzzed inbound parser + HMAC-SHA256/replay + rate-limit + real QUERY SHIELD closing SEC-039-for-queries + a less-privileged SEC-014 input process + the VIRGIN I211 RX bring-up); host-fuzzable-first ordering (the biggest CI win of the phase) + an M0 RX-feasibility spike that gates the arc + a hard-gated `JARVIS_CONTROL_IN` flip ("mostly gated" a NAMED-forbidden failure mode). **Pre-mortem-hardened by a 6-dimension adversarial-review WORKFLOW (24 verified findings, 9 HIGH) — the DIRECTION held but material corrections folded:** the SEC-014 containment was FALSE as first written (BAR0 + IOMMU-off = a bus-master DMA master = root-equivalent → PA keeps BAR0, the process gets RX buffer pages + a doorbell only); a cross-reboot/parser-crash replay hole (→ the sequence floor persists + the replay window is PA-side); the M0 spike would receive NOTHING (the guarded RX init omits `RXDCTL.ENABLE`, the TX-B2 analog); the action keyword-blocklist is a poor query filter (→ a query threat model, not the blocklist); the response LEAKS recalled memory (→ unicast to the console addr only); inbound Q&A is never WRITTEN to episodic (→ the done-when needs the control-IN write); the 6-3 trust seam is LATENT (no v1 TRUST_REQUEST action). 15 open questions for the strategist. Honest ceiling: a bounded, authenticated, rate-limited, SHIELD-scored two-way CONVERSATION — NOT a shell, NOT command execution, NOT the workspace arc; "not yet" is the correct default until "provably safe." **GOAL 6-5 COMPLETE — `JARVIS_CONTROL_IN` FLIPPED DEFAULT-ON 2026-07-21 (M4e). The deployed image is now TWO-WAY: the console can hold a bounded, authenticated, replay-protected, rate-limited, SHIELD-scored conversation with the box.** **SCOPE OF "COMPLETE": the SECURITY arc is complete and the channel is live; ONE §14 done-when is NOT met and is carried forward — the canon *multi-turn conversation referencing a fact from a PRIOR control-IN session*. That is a FUNCTIONALITY gap, not a testing gap: `pa_ctrl_gate` deliberately CLEARS the retrieval preamble for a control-IN query (`main_x86.c:2670-2672` — "retrieval-grounded control-IN is a later slice"), so the recall half is UNWIRED (the `EPI_ACT_CONTROL_IN` episodic WRITE half IS proven). Second carry-forward — **COMMENT CORRECTED IN PLACE 2026-07-26 (comment-only, zero behaviour change; `main.c.obj` `.text`/`.rodata`/`.data` + `nm` byte-identical, DWARF-only whole-file delta).** `main_x86.c`'s **`spine_decide()`** (cite the SYMBOL — the old citation `main_x86.c:2164` had ROTTED, 2164 is now inside the control-IN input-process spawn) passes `control_in_available=false` with what used to read "no control-IN yet". That comment went FALSE at the 6-5 flip (control-IN default-ON since 2026-07-21) — **but the `false` literal is still CORRECT and inert, and was deliberately NOT changed**: that argument is read by exactly ONE branch of `trust_policy` (`TRUST_REQUEST`, `shield_action.c`), and `action_allowlist.c` holds exactly **FOUR** entries, all `TRUST_AUTO` or `TRUST_NOTIFY` — no `TRUST_REQUEST`, no `TRUST_REQUIRE` — so the branch is **unreachable in the deployed build**. Wiring it to a live value would ALSO break `spine_decide`'s documented **"Pure: touches ZERO globals"** property, which was a deliberate outcome of the 6-1/M1 behaviour-neutral extract — a real design decision, not a tidy-up. The replacement comment states why the literal is inert AND what to wire it to when the first `TRUST_REQUEST` action lands: the same channel-up gate `TLM_F_CONTROL_IN` uses (`g_ctrl_key_ok && g_ctrl_floor_ok && g_ctrl_console_ok && g_ctrl_rx_ready`), with the purity property revisited at that point.** All 6 checklist items closed, `/security-review` CLEAN, emergency-disable proven. The flip ran TWICE by design: attempt 1 (boot_id=29, real provisioned key) validated the channel and **caught a real bug the earlier campaign structurally could not see** — the input process fed the token bucket a per-frame counter, so it accepted exactly 8 queries per boot then died (`rl=2` with parse/auth/replay all 0). Fails closed, no security hole, but it falsified the standing-channel claim ⇒ flip ABORTED, box reverted, bug fixed first (`bb3ffe9`, PA-stamped `fwd_ms` + a negative-control regression). The re-flip (boot_id=30) passed every leg: **15/15 sustained at human pace** (`acc=55` in one boot vs the old ceiling of 8), a 24-frame non-routing flood **limited at exactly CAP=8 with 16 dropped** then **3/3 recovery**, the browser round-trip coherent + HMAC-verified, `err=0`, audit-clean (49 EXECUTED + 21 BLOCKED, all 25 raw-query probes ZERO). Ubuntu keeps `BootOrder[0]` — control-IN is live only while JARVIS is deliberately booted. Rollback = the retained `=0` image + the JKEY wipe, both proven. **Honest limits carried forward:** the reply is SIGNED, NOT ENCRYPTED; the query SHIELD refuses DEFINED abuse classes and is NOT a general injection detector (containment is structural — K-b + the key never entering Process B); and third-host non-observation of the unicast reply remains **NOT PROVEN** (needs a wired capture point). Next: 6-6 (routing ≥95%) / 6-7 (the 7-day supervised exit). |

Proven, not assumed: modelling `epi_index_lookup`'s newest-wins-per-key over the CURRENT image with the SHIPPED C predicts **exactly 7 keys yielding `hit=1 len=0`**, and the run observed exactly 7. **So the absolute was repaired into an A/B on the same fixture** (the C/M1a "GATE 2 was mis-specified → repair it into GATE 2′, don't delete it" precedent): the **pre-change build `9254eff`** — the source of the image being replaced — was rebuilt and run against that same image and produced the **IDENTICAL** distribution (10× `hit=0`, 7× `hit=1 len=0`, 17 `[INFER]`, `err=0`). **That is what the criterion existed to prove: the default-ON workload lane is unmoved.** **GATED, NOT VALIDATED — stated plainly: KVM has no NIC, so the control-IN recall path CANNOT fire there** (the only `[CTRL-RECALL]` line is the boot-time index build, `n=14`). **TWO HARDWARE LEGS ARE OWED and are the operator's to run, and this deploy exists so both ride ONE boot rather than two:** (1) from `0aadb79` — `"explain paging in one line"` should give `[CTRL-RECALL] hit=1 recall=1 len=160` (was `len=0`), **and the load-bearing check is that the ANSWER IS COHERENT PROSE, not that `hit=1`** (a longer preamble is a bigger nudge and `f1fc84b` proved preamble shape can tip Gemma into `<|channel>thought`; `hit=1` with a thought dump is a FAILURE); (2) from `49d797f` — `"how long have you been up?"` → tag 4, `"what is your cpu usage?"` → tag 4, `"what is a page fault?"` → tag 3, read back with `sudo dd if=/dev/nvme0n1 bs=512 skip=21140000 count=4097 | python3 phase3/scripts/parse_episodic.py`. **The live control-IN key fingerprint is `37b420f0`** — start the receiver FRESH or a stale one will sign with the superseded key and the failure will look like a dead box. `JARVIS_THINKING` stays **0** in this image and was never part of it. **SUPERSEDED 2026-07-29 by the front-loading deploy — see the next block.** **DEPLOYED STATE UPDATED 2026-07-29 (T1b, commit `5584275` + this record): image md5 `93a549bc82dabfb09a26eae2c4e7afff`** (READ BACK off `/dev/nvme0n1p4` and byte-equal to the box-built binary — the only proof the ESP holds what was built rather than what was intended), **rollback `61e8142f02cf8a5301d8b7f8b74b302a`** (the recall-fix image, retained in BOTH locations and md5-verified BEFORE the write: on the ESP as `.bak-pre-t1` and at `~/jarvis_image.bak-pre-t1`), kernel unchanged `d22affe8`, Ubuntu keeps `BootOrder[0]` (`0001,0000`), **no `BootNext` remaining** (the one-shot consumed itself and the box returned to Ubuntu by itself). **SHIPPED (both ungated, control-IN ONLY):** #18 the front-loading instruction and #9 verdict-4 truncation reporting. **SUPERVISED BARE-METAL VALIDATION, boot_id=47:** all six operator questions answered, **`[CTRL-IN-STATS] acc=6 drop=0 (parse=0 rl=0 auth=0 replay=0) bp=0 down=0 recall=3`**, `err=0` the ONLY value that ever appears, 766/766 `[SNAP] model=loaded NN=6`, self-test `5/5 (3 real, 2 vacuous)`, and **0 `[FATAL]` / 0 `[MODEL-BAD]` / 0 `[ANOMALY]` / 0 `[RESTART]`**. Six `[CTRL-IN] ACCEPT` records match the six questions in order by length (26/21/69/26/23/21) on strictly ascending seqs. **ZERO `<|channel>`/thought text in any reply** — the §2c ordering (instruction AFTER the recall preamble, immediately before the question) holds on real hardware with a real preamble, which had only ever been shown in KVM. **Store tag split EXACTLY as predicted — 3/3/3/4/4/3** (`CONTROLIN` for the four model-answered, `CTRLIN-LOCAL` for the two PA-rendered SYSFACTS/DECLINE), all `/OK`. **THE VERDICT (the strategist's, NOT the operator's — the operator explicitly DECLINED to judge answer length, and attributing a judgement to someone who did not make one is the same class of error as a commit describing work never done): DEPLOYED, ALL REGRESSION CHECKS CLEAN, ANSWER LENGTH ACCEPTABLE BUT UNDER REVIEW.** The thin-answer risk (caveat 3) is real but NARROWER than feared — it shows on open explanatory/reasoning questions, not on "one line" questions. **Q3 is the weakest**: *"because a single-threaded program can only execute one task at a time"* is correct but CIRCULAR — it restates the question instead of explaining why the extra cores have no independent work to take. **Q6 is the evidence the ceiling is the INSTRUCTION, not the model**: asked twice, the second answer opened byte-identically to the first and then CONTINUED with new detail, which is recall firing on hardware and the model doing exactly what the preamble label asks. **`recall=3`, not 4, and the reason is verified rather than assumed:** of the four INFER questions, Q3's newest prior (`[12:00055]`) is a 256-B store-capped fragment with **ZERO sentence terminators**, so `g3_clean_answer_len`'s complete-sentence rule correctly emitted NO preamble — the suppression working as designed, not a recall failure. **VERDICT 4 IS NOT HARDWARE-EXERCISED — stated plainly rather than implied as validated:** all six real answers ended naturally, so #9 shipped correct and UNEXERCISED on the box; it remains KVM-proven only (a probe-gated `CAPPROBE` clamp forced the CAP stop there). **TWO MEASUREMENT LIMITS OF THE DEPLOY CONFIG, recorded so a later session does not mistake absence for evidence: (a) per-answer `n=` is NOT AVAILABLE** — `[PB] gen` is probe-gated and the boot emitted 0 of them, so the KVM `n≈38` figure for Q2 is NEITHER confirmed NOR refuted on hardware, and it must not be back-inferred from byte lengths; **(b) `[CTRL-IN-REPLY]` / `[CTRL-IN-RESP]` / `[CTRL-RECALL]` / `[ROUTE]` are plain `puts_serial` and are NOT durable at `BOOT_LOG=0`, so the ABSENCE of a `TRUNCATED` line is not evidence of no truncation.** No-verdict-4 is instead established from the answers themselves: Q1/Q2/Q3 stored at 166/186/180 B — all BELOW the 256-B `EPI_RESP_MAX` and all ending in a full stop — and Q6's verbatim reply ends on a complete sentence. **A CORRECTION TO THE "STRICTLY BETTER" FRAMING, since the evidence is weaker than it looks:** the pre-deploy Q2 record `[15:00065]` ends mid-clause, but it measures **exactly 256 B = the STORE cap**, so from the store alone a store-capped record is INDISTINGUISHABLE from a generation-truncated one. What IS provable: the post-deploy Q2 answer is **186 B, under the cap, ending in a full stop — complete as generated**. That the old one ran to the 250-token cap rests on the M2 KVM measurement, not on this record. **TWO PROCESS FINDINGS FROM THE DEPLOY ITSELF: (1) `install_jarvis_x86.sh` HAS NO NON-INTERACTIVE FLAG** (`--yes`/`--force`/`ASSUME_YES`/`NONINTERACTIVE`/`BATCH` all absent), so `guard_esp_device`'s two confirmations (`:524-527` — re-type the partition, then `ADD-JARVIS`) were satisfied BY MACHINE over ssh with **no sanctioned bypass**. The outcome here was sound (exact partition named in the prompt in advance, backups hashed first, read-back md5 matched) — but **a confirmation answered by machine is not a confirmation**: its purpose is to make a human look at the device path AT THE MOMENT OF THE WRITE, and a typo'd partition in a future prompt would no longer be caught. **RECORDED AS A KNOWN GAP; adding a `--yes` flag is its own change with its own review and was deliberately NOT done here.** **(2) `install_jarvis_x86.sh` is `-rwxr-xr-x` in the main-PC repo but `-rw-rw-r--` on the box clone**, so `./phase3/scripts/…` fails there with `command not found`; invoke it via `bash` rather than `chmod`-ing a tracked file (which would leave a spurious mode change in git).

## Architecture

### Model B: Microkernel with AI Control

```
Hardware Layer
    ↓
Microkernel (Ring 0, cores 0-1)
• Interrupt handling (<1ms)
• Memory management, IPC primitives
• ~12K LOC, seL4 (formally verified*)
    ↓ ← Lock-free IPC →
AI Decision Engine (Ring 3, cores 2-N)
• Specialist agents (4-6) — domain-expert agents (device, network, filesystem, user), NOT model-size tiers
• Decision cache (50ms→<1ms for 85% ops)
• SHIELD safety framework
    ↓
User Space Services (Ring 3)
• Device drivers, File systems, Applications
```

> *\*seL4 "(formally verified)" refers to its verified configurations. JARVIS's x86-64 build runs `KernelFastpath=ON`, which is **outside** the verified X64 config ("C-level functional correctness, **no fast path**") — so JARVIS runs a functional-but-unverified seL4 configuration by design (IPC/AVX perf > holding the proof). See `phase4/docs/PHASE_4_GOAL1_INFERENCE_PERF.md` §3.*

**Why:** AI inference (50-500ms) is 3 orders of magnitude too slow for Ring 0 interrupt handling (<1ms). Microkernel isolates time-critical ops from AI.

### Phase 2 Split Deployment (COMPLETE)

```
┌──────────────────┐       UART        ┌──────────────────┐
│   PC (Host)      │◄─────────────────►│   Pi 4 (seL4)    │
│                  │   115200 baud     │                  │
│  Python AI       │                   │  Decision Cache  │
│  - Phi-3 Mini    │   7ms median RTT  │  - 258 patterns  │
│  - Llama 3.2 1B  │                   │  - 85.7% hit     │
│  - SHIELD        │                   │  - <1ms lookup   │
└──────────────────┘                   └──────────────────┘
```

- **Cache hits (85%):** seL4 decision cache answers in <1ms
- **Cache misses (15%):** Forwarded to PC via UART (7ms RTT)
- **No Python on Pi 4:** seL4 userspace is C-only
- Phase 3+ returns to standalone (x86 or Multi-Pi cluster)

### Architecture Enhancements

- **Decision Cache:** Pre-compiled query→bytecode patterns. 135x speedup (50ms→<1ms), 85.7% hit rate.
- **SHIELD Safety:** Staged execution sandbox, continuous risk scoring (0-1.0), shadow execution, deterministic rollback.
- **Shared Context Pool:** Lock-free shared state for agent coordination. 220x faster than serialization.

### Key Constraints

**AI cannot:** handle interrupts (<1ms), generate drivers real-time, run at Ring 0
**AI can:** make high-level decisions (50-500ms), optimize configs, coordinate agents, learn over time

## Coding Conventions & Patterns

### C/Python IPC Struct Alignment

Python ctypes structs MUST exactly match C struct layout:

```python
class RingMessage(ctypes.Structure):
    _pack_ = 1  # REQUIRED: No padding
    _fields_ = [
        ("type", ctypes.c_uint32),
        ("id", ctypes.c_uint32),
        ("payload_size", ctypes.c_uint32),
        ("payload", ctypes.c_char * MAX_MESSAGE_SIZE),
        ("timestamp", ctypes.c_uint64)
    ]
```

### Query Normalization

Both C and Python must normalize identically: lowercase → collapse spaces → trim → FNV-1a 64-bit hash.
- C: `cache_normalize_query()` in `decision_cache.c`
- Python: `QueryProcessor.normalize_query()` in `query_processor.py`

### seL4 Tutorials CMakeLists.txt Pattern

```cmake
include(${SEL4_TUTORIALS_DIR}/settings.cmake)
sel4_tutorials_regenerate_tutorial(${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR})
include(rootserver)
include(simulation)
add_executable(hello-world src/main.c ...)
target_link_libraries(hello-world sel4 muslc utils sel4muslcsys sel4platsupport sel4utils sel4debug)
DeclareRootserver(hello-world)
```

Note: `DeclareTutorialApp()` does NOT exist. Use `add_executable()` + `DeclareRootserver()`.

### Testing Philosophy

- Every component has standalone tests printing PASS/FAIL
- C tests: compile and run directly (no deps)
- Python tests: `python test_*.py` (mock mode if seL4 unavailable)
- Always aim for 100% pass rate

### Development Documentation Pattern

- **Phase 2** used weekly status files: `phase2/weeks/weekN/WEEK_N_STATUS.md` (weeks 27-49), committed weekly with the week number in the message.
- **Phase 3** was documented via topic/milestone docs in `phase3/docs/` (KICKOFF, HARDWARE_RESEARCH, IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN, the QEMU/native test-results docs, MODEL_BENCH_OFF, the security audits), the ADRs in `docs/decisions/`, and `phase3/docs/PHASE_3_FINAL_REPORT.md` — **not** weekly status files (there is no `phase3/weeks/`).
- **Phase 4** resumes weekly status files at `phase4/weeks/weekN/WEEK_N_STATUS.md` (like Phase 2); Phase 3 used topic docs.
- Implement, test, update the relevant doc; commit with a descriptive subject referencing the milestone/ADR where applicable.

### Bare-Metal Development Rules

- **Always test in QEMU before flashing USB** — run `phase3/scripts/qemu_test.sh` after every build. If it crashes in QEMU, don't waste time reflashing. **The script IS the gate invocation (fixed 2026-07-25): it derives `-smp` from the BUILT kernel config, attaches the NVMe image, and defaults to `-snapshot`.** Before that fix it passed **no `-smp` and attached no drive at all** (handing the model to seL4 as an initrd module — the ABANDONED path), so it booted non-SMP with no model and a green run proved nothing; `qemu_test_nvme.sh` is now a deprecated shim that forwards here. **Image size is load-bearing:** the raw-LBA stores begin at LBA 21,100,000 (10.06 GiB), so an image smaller than ~10.1 GiB cannot contain them and every store silently fails to init — the script now REFUSES such an image instead. The durable telemetry log at LBA 4,000,794,624 (1907 GiB) is not KVM-reachable at any practical size; read it on the box.
- **Build without embedded model for fast iteration** — use `-DJARVIS_EMBED_MODEL=""` for quick boot tests. Model loading is tested separately via the NVMe FAT32 runtime path (the GRUB-module approach was abandoned — seL4 overwrites module memory during rootserver relocation).
- **Verify GRUB menu entry works** — wrong image names cause silent boot failures (keyboard dies, no output). Check grub.cfg image names match `ls ~/sel4-x86/jbuild/images/`.
- **All on-screen UI MUST mirror to the NVMe log (verify UI from the log, not photos)** — the box is headless-except-monitor and showing on-screen UI to a reviewer is impractical and gets worse as the UI grows. So every render routes through a single log-mirror chokepoint (e.g. `fb_status_line` → `[PANEL] <text>`); emit a periodic full-UI snapshot (all fields/widgets in one block) at init + a steady cadence; add a boot-relative timestamp (TSC→ms) so the log shows what **and when**. "UI state fully reconstructable from the log" is an **exit criterion** for every goal-#2+ UI slice. Caveat: the log proves **content, not pixels** — do ONE visual confirm per rendering/font/layout change, then trust the log. Gotcha: anything drawn before `nvme_log_init` is serial-only (re-emit it via the post-init snapshot).
- **UI must track real features (the UI is the living truth-map)** — every user-visible feature/capability MUST be reflected in the UI: primarily the **Remote Telemetry Console** (`phase4/console/`, fed by the `/events` SSE telemetry), secondarily the on-box HUD. When a feature ships, either surface its **real live signal** on the relevant console screen, or expose it in the console's auto-populated **Capabilities/Features** section (driven from real telemetry / a declared feature manifest — never hardcoded claims). Honesty corollary: the UI shows ONLY real, live state — a feature that exists but is missing from the UI is a **gap to close**; anything shown without a live source is **fiction to remove**. So "what's working vs what isn't (or isn't meant to be there)" stays obvious to both the user and the AI at a glance. A new feature means updating the console (or its feature section) **in the same change**; enforced by the console honesty gate (`phase4/console/test_console_honesty.py`).

## Current Status (Phase 3 — Process-Isolated LLM Inference on seL4)

**NVMe RUNTIME MODEL LOADING MILESTONE** (April 4, 2026) — **LLM inference with runtime NVMe model loading on bare-metal seL4 on Ryzen 7 2700X.** 1.6MB USB boot image loads the model GGUF from the NVMe FAT32 partition (Lexar NM790, PCI bus 1) at runtime — model-agnostic, loading whatever `JARVIS_MODEL_FILE` names (current default `"GEMMA2B GUF"` = Gemma 4 E2B Q4_K_M, ~2.89 GiB). NVMe driver: PCI detection → BAR0 MMIO → admin queue → IDENTIFY → I/O queue → FAT32 read → frames → Process B. (Original Apr 4 milestone: 770MB Llama 3.2 1B Q4_K_M → 197K frames.) Coherent text generation via process-isolated IPC. No embedded model binary. Self-test 5/5 PASS, 181 untypeds, 32410MB RAM.

Design pre-mortem-hardened by 3 adversarial-review WORKFLOWS (opus/high, ~33 realistic FPs folded: the systemic bare-possessive→emit-anchor retune, the "system prompt" LLM-dev family, the design-opinion gap-4 class). **M3-1-era status was HOST + CI ONLY; SHIPPED LIVE at the 6-5 flip 2026-07-21 — every validated inbound query is scored here before PA routes it (M3-2 wired the box route + response; telemetry/console landed at M3-4). Unit 87 asserts, FP 0/100, fuzz 300,000 iters.** |
| Query router (Phase 6 6-6/B/M0, host) | — | DONE | route.c/h, routing_suite.h, test_route.c, fuzz_route.c | host CI: 2 steps — `route_classify(q,len)` → ROUTE_{INFER,SYSFACTS,DECLINE} (pure keyword intent classifier over a DEFINED handler set; the query_shield.c precedent — length-carried, NEVER strlen, host-pure, crash/UB-free for any bytes) + `sysfacts_answer` over a host-WHITELISTED `sysfacts_t` (uptime/boot_id/model/nodes/health ONLY — a `_Static_assert` sizeof/offsetof pins the whitelist so a new field is a conscious edit; the load-generator counters q_total/q_hits/q_errors + all internal/security state are DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED, §4) + a canned DECLINE for no-source status metrics (cpu/disk/time/temp/memory-used — NEVER fabricated). **test_route 109 asserts** (unit per-field/decline/conservative-boundary + adversarial-byte crash-freedom + the sysfacts_t structural whitelist pin + the ACCURACY gate) + **300K-iter ASan/UBSan fuzz** (RAW exact-len-heap + STRUCTURED + sysfacts_answer bounded) → CI "Phase 6: 6-6/B0 Query router (C)" + "…Fuzz (C, ASAN)". **THE ≥95% IS KEYWORD-BLIND:** `routing_suite.h`'s HELDOUT split was authored by an INDEPENDENT pass with NO sight of route.c's keyword tables (route.c did not exist); route.c was tuned against the DEV split ONLY and HELDOUT run once — **HELDOUT 64/67 = 95.52% on the FIRST run, no iteration; DEV 100%; 0 INFER-misroutes** (no general query ever sent to a wrong fact — the conservative property). The 3 misses are typo probes ("uptiem"/"wokers"/"memry") that fail SAFE toward INFER — the honest ceiling of a keyword (non-semantic) router, and the argument for the §8 embedder arc, not a reason to widen keywords. HOST + CI ONLY — no box wiring, no `JARVIS_ROUTING` flag (B/M1 wires it into `pa_ctrl_gate`). |

The assert figure remains a SELF-REPORTED sum of per-suite tallies, not a measured total: the previous ~790 plus ~440 from the 6-5 C suites alone (parser 43 + replay 23 + ratelimit 13 + verify 22 + floor 48 + console 70 + reply 86 + signer-differential 47 + query SHIELD 87, on top of the SHA-256/HMAC KAT steps).)*

**Phase 3b on real hardware — bare-metal burn-in passed (2026-06-15, NOT a 30-day soak):** clean boot, NVMe model load (LBA-32768), coherent Gemma 4 E2B, heartbeat/shield IPC fix holding at err=0 over 400 queries (q=100/200/300/400), durable NVMe logging with constant boot_id. Formal 30-day x86 soak **DEFERRED — descoped from v0.2.1-beta gating (risk-accepted)**: the new x86 surfaces (shmem_ipc, in-process inference, NVMe load) are covered by this ~400-query burn-in + 300K-iter fuzz + 2 audits + the IPC fix; Phase 2's 30.6-day Pi 4 run (different IPC/ARM stack) is supporting context, not equivalent coverage. See `docs/decisions/2026-06-15-defer-30-day-x86-stability-soak.md`.

## Key Technical Notes

### UART IPC Protocol

```
┌──────────┬──────────┬──────────┬──────────┬──────────┬──────────┬──────────┐
│  SYNC    │  TYPE    │   SEQ    │  LENGTH  │  FLAGS   │ PAYLOAD  │  CRC16   │
│ (2 bytes)│ (1 byte) │ (2 bytes)│ (2 bytes)│ (1 byte) │ (0-240)  │ (2 bytes)│
│  0xAA55  │  0x01-0E │  0-65535 │  0-240   │  0x00    │  data    │ CRC-CCITT│
└──────────┴──────────┴──────────┴──────────┴──────────┴──────────┴──────────┘
```

| Type | Value | Direction | Description |
|------|-------|-----------|-------------|
| QUERY | 0x01 | Py→seL4 | Cache lookup |
| RESPONSE | 0x02 | seL4→Py | Cache result |
| HEARTBEAT | 0x03 | Both | Keep-alive |
| HEARTBEAT_ACK | 0x04 | Both | Keep-alive ack |
| STATS_REQUEST | 0x05 | Py→seL4 | Cache stats req |
| STATS_RESPONSE | 0x06 | seL4→Py | Cache stats |
| COMMAND | 0x07 | Py→seL4 | Shell command |
| COMMAND_RESULT | 0x08 | seL4→Py | Command output |
| SHIELD_CHECK | 0x09 | Py→seL4 | Risk assessment |
| SHIELD_RESULT | 0x0A | seL4→Py | Risk decision |
| ERROR | 0x0B | Both | Error |
| RESET | 0x0C | Both | Protocol reset |
| STATE_CHANGE | 0x0D | Py→seL4 | State change |
| STATE_ACK | 0x0E | seL4→Py | State ack |

Baud: 115200. RTT: 7ms measured. Heartbeat: 5s. Timeout: 30s.
Full spec: `phase2/docs/UART_IPC_PROTOCOL.md`

### BCM2711 Hardware

```
GENET Ethernet:  0xFD580000  (separate device untyped!)
Peripheral Base: 0xFE000000
System Timer:    0xFE003000  (1 MHz free-running counter)
GPIO:            0xFE200000
UART0 (PL011):   0xFE201000
EMMC/SDHCI:      0xFE340000
BSC1 I2C:        0xFE804000  (I2C master, 100/400 kHz)
DWC2 USB:        0xFE980000  (USB OTG host controller)
```

USB-Serial wiring: GPIO14(TXD)→RXD, GPIO15(RXD)←TXD, GND─GND

### SD Card Boot (U-Boot)

```
Boot Partition (FAT32):
├── start4.elf      # GPU firmware
├── fixup4.dat      # Memory config
├── u-boot.bin      # U-Boot 2026.01 bootloader
├── boot.scr        # U-Boot boot script
├── kernel8.img     # JARVIS seL4 boot image
├── bcm2711-rpi-4-b.dtb  # Device tree
└── config.txt      # arm_64bit=1, kernel=u-boot.bin, enable_uart=1

Boot flow: GPU firmware → U-Boot → boot.scr → kernel8.img → seL4
```

U-Boot: Press key during 3s countdown for interactive shell. Auto-boot loads kernel8.img at 0x00080000.
Backup: `temp_sd_backup/uboot_working/`

### seL4 Device Mapping Rules

- **Forward-only cursor:** seL4 Untyped_Retype watermark only moves forward. Map devices in ascending paddr order.
- **VSpace range:** vaddr must be within mapped VSpace (0x400000-0x5b9fff). Error 6 = seL4_FailedLookup means vaddr not in VSpace.
- **Init order:** systimer(0xFE003000) → DMA(0xFE007000) → mailbox(0xFE00B000) → watchdog(0xFE100000) → RNG(0xFE104000) → UART/GPIO(0xFE200000-0xFE201000) → SPI(0xFE204000) → PWM(0xFE20C000) → EMMC(0xFE340000) → I2C(0xFE804000) → USB(0xFE980000)
- **Binary buddy skip:** When timer is mapped before UART, use power-of-2 Untyped retypes to advance watermark from 0xFE004000→0xFE200000 (7 retypes: 16KB→32KB→64KB→128KB→256KB→512KB→1MB) instead of 2MB LargePage skip (which would consume GPIO's frame).
- **Device cursor after mapping:** GPIO_BASE + 2*4KB = 0xFE202000
- **DMA = uncacheable:** seL4 does NOT set `SCTLR_EL1.UCI`, so ALL cache maintenance instructions (`DC IVAC/CIVAC/CVAC`) trap from EL0. Map DMA buffers with `vm_attributes = 0` (uncacheable) instead.

### Virtual Address Layout

```
0x5c0000 - UART PL011 (hardcoded)
0x5c1000 - GPIO (hardcoded)
0x5c2000 - System Timer (auto-assigned)
0x5c3000 - EMMC (auto-assigned)
0x5c4000-0x603FFF - DMA pool (256KB)
0x604000-0x609FFF - GENET MMIO (6 pages, own device untyped)
0x610000 - VideoCore Mailbox (explicit vaddr, maps 0xFE00B000)
0x611000 - PM Watchdog (explicit vaddr, maps 0xFE100000)
0x612000 - DMA Engine (explicit vaddr, maps 0xFE007000)
RNG, SPI, PWM - auto-assigned (0xFE104000, 0xFE204000, 0xFE20C000)
0x60A000-0x60CFFF - DWC2 USB (3 pages, paddr 0xFE980000)
```

### Phase 1 IPC Limitation

Phase 1 used "mock IPC" - Python and seL4 did NOT communicate in real-time. Separate memory spaces. Both proven independently, connected in Phase 2 via UART.

### NVMe Write Logging

- Log region: LBA 4000794624 (past p4, the 1.7G vfat tail partition). Drive confirmed via lsblk: **Lexar NM790 2TB**, 4,000,797,360 sectors (1907 GiB). The log's 2701 sectors (header + 2700 entries) fit the ~2,736-sector tail with only ~35 sectors (~18 KB) of headroom — TIGHT. (nvme_log.h's header comments match — "Lexar NM790 2TB", "~2,736-sector tail, ~35 sectors spare".)
- Max entries: 2700 — **circular / rolling buffer** (keeps the latest 2700; never fills): `cursor` is the wrapping write slot (0..2699), `total_entries` is the monotonic lifetime count, and `nvme_log_cursor()` returns entries-stored-capped (rolling-full once total ≥ 2700). `nvme_log_write` no longer returns -2; a stale pre-wrap cursor (== 2700 from the old no-wrap build) is clamped on init. The telemetry `log_cursor` field value/shape is **unchanged** by this (identical until the first wrap) — no wire-contract change.
- Periodic stats cadence: serial `[STATS]` prints every 100 queries (free); the NVMe `LOG_IPC_STATS` entry is written every `JARVIS_STATS_NVME_INTERVAL` = **100** queries (`jarvis_debug.h`). Justified by the **measured** bare-metal rate ~3k queries/day (single core, scalar) → ~870 entries over 30 days, well under the 2700-entry cap. (Task 3 originally used 1000 on a 16k/day estimate the bare-metal run disproved; q=400 err=0 verified on real hardware 2026-06-15. If a future build speeds the query rate up ~5x+, raise this back toward 1000.)
- Format: 512-byte records (header + entries), XOR checksum
- Auto-capture: `puts_serial()` intercept writes every line to NVMe log
- Tags: `[VGA]` = visible on monitor, `[SER]` = serial only, `[PB]` = Process B via MSG_DEBUG IPC
- Recovery: `sudo dd if=/dev/nvme0n1 bs=512 skip=4000794624 count=2701 | python3 phase3/scripts/parse_nvme_log.py` (count=2701 = header + all 2700 slots; the parser reads wrap-order — oldest→newest from `cursor` — once the buffer has rolled)
- `seL4_Yield` only yields to equal-priority — PA and PB both at MaxPrio

### Bare-Metal Debugging Rules

- Use `#if EMMC_DEBUG` preprocessor guards for diagnostics, NOT code deletion (deleting debug code caused mysterious boot failures)
- `seL4_DebugPutChar()` works for TX without device frame mapping
- Device frame mapping required for UART RX
- Serial console: 115200 baud, 8N1
- `put_dec()` and `put_hex()` go through `putc_serial()` -> NVMe log. To suppress NVMe logging temporarily (e.g. verbose dumps), save/clear `g_nvme_ptr`.
- Process A and Process B run at `seL4_MaxPrio` (equal priority). `seL4_Yield` only works between equal-priority threads — MaxPrio-1 gets starved.
- Response ring has 15 slots. **`pb_can_log()` reserves 3 for MSG_RESPONSE — but this is INTENT, NOT SHIPPED FACT** (verified 2026-07-25, C/M1b pre-mortem): `pb_can_log` is `__attribute__((unused))` (`inference_server.c:151`) and its SOLE call site (`:164`) is inside `#if JARVIS_DBG_PB`, which is **default 0** — so in the deployed build the debug sends compile out entirely and **no ring reservation is enforced at runtime**. Harmless today (nothing competes), but it means there is **no existing backpressure mechanism to lean on**: the MSG_RESPONSE chunk loop DISCARDS `shmem_ipc_send`'s return code (`(void)rc`, `inference_server.c:500-516`) while that function returns −1 on a full ring, so a full ring would silently drop a chunk and the loop would advance. Latent only because `char text_out[512]` (`:479`) caps a response at ≤3 chunks. **Any future bulk payload (e.g. a 1024-float embedding = 18 chunks) must NOT go through this ring** — see `phase6/docs/PHASE_6_GOAL_C_M1B_DESIGN.md` §4.5.
- `qmodel_forward` stack budget: keep below ~8KB. Large temporaries (>4KB) MUST use `state->fwd_scratch` heap buffer. seL4 Process B has limited stack.
- **PA response consumption: POLL the ring, do NOT `seL4_Wait(resp_notif)` in the workload loop.** The inference path polls and never consumes `resp_notif`, leaving it signaled; heartbeat/shield used to `seL4_Wait(resp_notif)` + a single recv, which returned immediately on that stale signal and read before PB responded (~7% spurious errors, all in hb/shield). Fixed via `wait_for_response()` which polls the ring for the expected type and drains stale messages (race-free, like inference). The only legit `seL4_Wait(resp_notif)` is the pre-loop ready handshake. NOTE: the race is **Heisenbug** — enabling `JARVIS_DBG_IPC` serial prints inserts a scheduling window that masks it, so verify by err-rate ([STATS]) at `JARVIS_DBG_IPC=0`, not per-event logs.
