ON = 900 tok **`stop=CAP`**, **think=828 answer=72** — cut at *"Your current system is explicitly **memory-"*, so **the pre-registered verdict cannot even be APPLIED to what was delivered**. **THE MECHANISM IS THE FINDING: the thought does not REPLACE the answer's reasoning, it DUPLICATES it.** The model works the problem in full inside the channel and then works it again in the answer, so the mode costs ~2× for the SAME content and at any finite budget the ANSWER is what gets truncated — both ON legs hit `stop=CAP` at 900 while both OFF legs ended NATURALLY at 496/866. **STEP 1's ~320-TOKEN SIZING DOES NOT GENERALISE, AND IS CORRECTED HERE:** think length scales with question difficulty — **320** (retrieval) → **651** (arithmetic) → **828** (multi-factor reasoning) — so a fixed think budget cannot be sized, and the harder the question (exactly where thinking would be worth having) the more it starves the answer. **D2 force-close is not a rescue**: closing at ~150 would truncate a thought needing 651–828 mid-reasoning, and `f1fc84b` already measured that a mid-clause prompt tips this model into garbage. **THE REASON IS THE BASELINE, not a defect in the mode — `THINKING=0` ALREADY EMITS VISIBLE CHAIN-OF-THOUGHT IN THE ANSWER BODY** (Q1 walked all five arithmetic steps; Q2 split into scenarios and explicitly asked *"Why not 50×?"*) and got **BOTH** pre-registered verdicts right in fewer total tokens than the ON thought ALONE consumed. **A FOURTH BLOCKER, measured incidentally: the default-ON WORKLOAD lane yields `answer=0` under THINKING=1, 3/3** (`[PB] gen n=50 cap=50 think=50 answer=0 closer=none`) — its 50-token budget is entirely eaten by a thought that never closes, so a THINKING flip is **not a control-IN-only decision**; it would silently empty the workload lane and every `[INFER]` with it. **RECOMMENDATION: the arc (separate budgets + D4 stripping + liveness tick + multi-frame, 3–4 days) is NOT worth building.** It buys elaboration, not correctness, on the two questions designed to reward reasoning; it costs ~2×; and it cannot be sized. **`JARVIS_THINKING` STAYS 0 — now on VALUE, not merely on cost.** Reopen ONLY if a question class is found where OFF is measurably WRONG and ON is right; neither pre-registered probe produced one. **Two side-findings from the same run: (a)** Q2's OFF answer is **3558 B = 2.5× the 1426 B transport ceiling**, so multi-frame replies are ALREADY binding on ordinary explanatory answers and are **not** a thinking-mode prerequisite; **(b)** the pre-registered Q1 had to be REWORDED because `route.c` captured it as SYSFACTS — see the `JARVIS_ROUTING` row's bare-`"faults"` finding. Arithmetic was preserved exactly (*"A page fault costs 10ms."* byte-identical; 40000 × 10 ms over a 2 hour run), so the verdict is unchanged; the deviation is recorded rather than glossed. **→ FULL RESEARCH + VERDICT: `phase6/docs/THINKING_MODE_RESEARCH.md` (2026-07-27). One line: CLOSED — `JARVIS_THINKING` stays 0 for THIS model on THIS workload, on convergent evidence from our own pre-registered A/B, the workload composition, and published evaluation of the family at this size (GSM8K 70%→55% WITH thinking at 2B; E2B's own AIME 2026 is 37.5%, not the 89.2% headline, which belongs to family members we do not run). Explicitly NOT a claim that thinking modes don't work — §5. Reopens on a larger/reasoning-trained model, a GPU, or a workload shift.** That doc also carries the **bench-off harness finding** (§7) and a §2 correction worth knowing before citing it: the "16 of 30 questions ask for one line" figure is numerically exact but **only ~4–5 of those are genuine operator questions** — the rest is our own probe/filler traffic, so it is corroboration, not "the empirical crux".
  - Committed defaults ARE the stability config: STATS + INFER_SUMMARY + **G3_RETRIEVAL** (default-ON since G3/M6, 2026-07-02) + **CACHE_GROWTH** (default-ON since #6/M3, 2026-07-03) + **ACTIONS** (default-ON since K/M4, 2026-07-08 — the live self-heal action gate; SEC-039 closed for the action path) + **MONITORS** (default-ON since 6-1 flip 2026-07-09 — the 4 always-on watchers, live over v8 telemetry) + **WAKE** (default-ON since 6-2 flip 2026-07-12 — the event-driven wake lane, live over v9 telemetry; honest-0 on a healthy box) + **PROACTIVE** (default-ON since 6-3 flip 2026-07-13 — the ≥5 registry INFORM behaviors + the calibrated 6/hr global cap, live over v10 telemetry; a healthy box fires ~only the uptime digests) + **CONTROL_IN** (default-ON since the 6-5 FLIP 2026-07-21 — the two-way conversation channel is LIVE whenever JARVIS runs; v11 telemetry, three fail-closed gates, Ubuntu keeps `BootOrder[0]` so exposure is bounded to deliberate JARVIS boots) + **CONTROL_IN_RECALL** (default-ON since the M5-recall FLIP 2026-07-22 — the box recalls a repeated control-IN query's own prior answer across sessions via the dedicated control-IN store @21,140,000, three-gate hardware-proven; EXACT-REPEAT scope, semantic association out of scope) + **ROUTING** (default-ON since the 6-6 B FLIP 2026-07-23 — the control-IN query router; SYSFACTS from PA state / DECLINE canned / INFER to the model, HELDOUT 95.89% keyword-blind) on, IPC/PB/RING/BOOT_LOG/G3_PROBE/G3_AB/SHIELD_LEARN/SHIELD_PROBE/**ACTION_PROBE/KM2A_SPIKE/MONITOR_PROBE/WAKE_PROBE/PROACTIVE_PROBE/CONTROL_IN_PROBE/CONTROL_IN_RECALL_PROBE/ROUTING_PROBE/MODEL_FAIL_PROBE** off (every *_PROBE stays off — the deploy never induces synthetic events) (BOOT_LOG off avoids NVMe write wear over 30 days; SHIELD_LEARN is #5's monitor-only risk map — flip is an M4 call; **ACTION_PROBE stays off — the deploy self-heals real faults but induces no synthetic ones; the QUERY path stays passive/ALLOW by design**)
- **NVMe Driver:** `phase3/src/drivers/nvme.c/h` — **+ `nvme_flush` (6-5/M4c-fix, GATED `#if JARVIS_CONTROL_IN`, control-IN ONLY):** NVM FLUSH (opcode 0x00) commits the drive's volatile write cache to NAND, making the control-IN replay-floor write power-safe. FLUSH deliberately, NOT FUA — FLUSH is MANDATORY in the NVMe spec (always honored) while the FUA write bit is OPTIONAL (a drive may treat such a write as normal). A SEPARATE command by design: `nvme_write_sectors` is textually unchanged, so the deployed write path (episodic / JACT / semantic) is byte-unchanged and OFF-identity holds trivially (the gate rides the build script's `-DJARVIS_CONTROL_IN=1` — this driver does not include `jarvis_debug.h`, the `nic_i211.c` precedent). **That gate is ON in the deployed image since the 6-5 flip 2026-07-21, so `nvme_flush` is LIVE on the replay-floor write.** Scoped to the floor only; the logs stay best-effort unflushed.
- **NVMe Log:** `phase3/src/drivers/nvme_log.c/h` — raw sector logging for bare-metal telemetry
- **NVMe Log Parser:** `phase3/scripts/parse_nvme_log.py` — extract + format log from raw device
- **NVMe Log Reset:** `phase3/scripts/clear_nvme_log.sh` — zero the telemetry-log region (derives LBA/count from `nvme_log.h`; archive first, `--yes` to write)
- **FAT32 Parser:** `phase3/src/drivers/fat32.c/h` — read-only FAT32; **FAT-sector cache** (`fat32_fs_t.fat_cache` — `fat32_next_cluster` reads each FAT sector once vs up to ~128× per chain → ~160 MB less NVMe I/O on the Gemma load) + a `fat32_read_file` **progress hook** (`fat32_fs_t.progress` → exact data-only load %). `fat32_init` already rejects sizes outside {512,1024,2048,4096} (SEC-028); 1024/2048/4096 are accepted but unsupported by the 512-byte `fat_cache` and fail safely (SEC-029 returns -1 at the first FAT read). Optional tightening: reject `!=512` outright. Never fires on the box's 512B NVMe. **fat32_read_file final partial-sector overflow fixed (H3)** — the trailing partial sector is read into a stack bounce buffer and only the leftover `tail` bytes copied, so it never writes up to bytes_per_sector-1 bytes past `buf+file_size` (delivers exactly file_size; deployed Gemma load byte-identical, only the unwritten junk-tail changes); regression in `test_fat32.c` (0xAA canary, single- + multi-cluster sub-sector files; fail pre-fix).
- **Fuzz Harness:** `phase3/src/drivers/fuzz_harness.c`
- **Security Audit (Apr 2026):** `phase3/docs/SECURITY_AUDIT_2026-04-06.md`
- **Model Bench-Off:** `phase3/docs/MODEL_BENCH_OFF_2026-04-07.md` — tiers, compatibility, deferred contenders (Gemma 4 §7, Qwen3.5 §7b, Qwen3 §9)
- **Bench Scripts:**
  - `phase3/scripts/bench_models.sh` — unified bench (speed/perplexity/quality) for JARVIS PC. **A PREFIXED quality run now REQUIRES `QUALITY_PREFIX_TAG` and FAILS CLOSED (exit 2, writes nothing) without it** — a prefix changes the system being measured, so it must not share an output filename. The old path used `${PREFIX:+.frontload}`, which collapsed every non-empty prefix to ONE name; the 2026-07-28 front-load-only run overwrote the two-clause run in place and those per-model artifacts were unrecoverable. The output name now derives from the tag. An unprefixed run is unaffected.
  - `phase3/scripts/bench_engine_models.sh` — JARVIS engine bench (all 11 models, quantized path)
  - `phase3/scripts/bench_speed_windows.ps1` — Windows speed bench (CPU + GPU modes)
  - `phase3/scripts/bench_perplexity_windows.ps1` — Windows perplexity bench (GPU, full WikiText-2)
- **Bench Results:** `models/bench_results/bench_results.txt` (JARVIS PC speed), `models/bench_results/bench_results_mainpc.txt` (5600 CPU), `models/bench_results/bench_results_mainpc_gpu.txt` (5600 + RTX 2070)
- **Engine Bench Results:** `models/bench_results/jarvis_engine_bench.txt` (JARVIS engine, 11/11 models, quantized path, 6 model families)

- **x86 Build Script:** `phase3/scripts/build_jarvis_x86.sh`
- **QEMU NVMe Test (CANONICAL):** `phase3/scripts/qemu_test.sh` — `bash qemu_test.sh [--image PATH] [--log FILE] [--smp N] [--mem 8G] [--no-snapshot]`. Defaults: image `~/nvme_test.img`, `-smp` DERIVED from the built kernel's `CONFIG_MAX_NUM_NODES` (so the runner cannot drift from the thing it runs), `-snapshot` ON (a gate never mutates the shared image, and two gates can run concurrently). Use `--log` for detached/`systemd-run` runs and `--no-snapshot` only when persistence is the point (e.g. a 2-boot store-survival test). **It no longer takes a model path** — the model is loaded at runtime from FAT32 as `GEMMA2B.GUF` on the image. `qemu_test_nvme.sh` is a DEPRECATED shim that forwards here and refuses a model-path argument.
- **QEMU NVMe Disk:** `~/nvme_test.img` FAT32 with the model file named to match `JARVIS_MODEL_FILE` (currently `GEMMA2B GUF`); `-drive file=...,if=none,id=nvme0,format=raw -device nvme,serial=QEMU_NVME,drive=nvme0`
- **Inference Server (PB):** `phase3/src/sel4/inference_server.c` — **STOP-TOKEN FIX 2026-07-26 (ungated, deployed path):** the generation loop capped at 50 tokens and broke ONLY on `<eos>`=1, with a comment that deliberately rejected the model's declared terminator (*"Stop on <eos>=1, NOT eos_id=106 (that's `<turn|>` which model emits first)"*). That had it backwards — **`<turn|>` IS the model declaring its turn finished**, `tokenizer.ggml.eos_token_id`=**106** is in the GGUF, `gguf_vocab.c` already reads it and `tokenizer_init` already copies it to `tok->eos_id`. The value was in memory and ignored, so a finished answer kept generating to the cap. The loop now breaks on `tok->eos_id` as well as `<eos>`, using the **LOADED** value (hardcoding ids is the habit that produced this bug class), and breaks **BEFORE storing** — a terminator is not answer text, and storing it is why records/replies carried a trailing `<turn|><turn|><eos>` and why that junk reached the recall corpus. `n_gen` therefore counts ANSWER tokens only. **MEASURED, and it corrected the estimate that motivated it:** a native-engine probe over 5 realistic questions with no preamble found **4 of 5 emit NO terminator at all** (the model never finishes inside 50 tokens, so there is nothing to pad) and 1 of 5 recovered 4 tokens (**8%**). The earlier "23 of 50 = 46% padding" figure came from ONE question **with a preamble injected**, which made the model finish early — it does not generalise. **The real finding is the inverse: 5 of 5 hit the 50-token cap, every answer truncated mid-sentence, so the token budget is the binding constraint and this fix barely moves it** (see `phase6/docs/ANSWER_QUALITY_DESIGN.md`). KVM gate: `q=100 hits=71 infer=13 err=0`, NN=6, coherent `[INFER]`, and **zero `turn|`/`<eos>` in the log vs one visible `turn|><turn|>` response tail in the pre-fix run**. Shared loop, so the workload lane gets the same fix. **Risk considered:** an early `<turn|>` would truncate a genuine answer — but emitting it IS the model ending its turn, and empirically it only ever appeared AFTER a completed answer; a model that ends its turn early is a model that has finished. **#18 FRONT-LOADING + #9 TRUNCATION-REPORTING 2026-07-28 (ungated, deployed path, CONTROL-IN ONLY):** `handle_query` now takes the lane **EXPLICITLY** (`is_control_in`, set from the same `MSG_QUERY_LONG` test that picks the cap) and injects `PB_FRONTLOAD_INSTRUCTION` = *"Answer the question directly in your first sentence."* (~10 tok) immediately before the question. **The lane is a parameter and NOT inferred from `max_tokens`** — a `max_tokens == 250` test would be a coincidence, not a fact, and the day the two caps matched it would silently front-load the WORKLOAD and re-base the 5.46 tok/s record with no error anywhere. **Measured blind, same rubric same run: E2B 9.0 vs Llama 3.1 8B 7.8** (E2B baseline 4.3) — the incumbent beats the nominal bench winner, so **no model swap**. **EXACTLY ONE CLAUSE:** the two-clause variant (*", then elaborate."*) was NET-HARMFUL in `1df800a` (control length-violations 0.0→7.0, quality 8.0→4.0) — do not re-add it. Three placement findings, each of which would have been a defect: the instruction is **outside** the `#if JARVIS_G3_RETRIEVAL`/`g_sctx_pb`/`pre_len>0` nest (inside it, it would vanish on every retrieval miss — common by design — and entirely in a G3=0 build); `g3_prompt_budget` now **reserves** for it (it derives room from `n_prompt` at call time, and the instruction is appended after, so the preamble could be granted room the instruction then consumed); and the bound check uses a new lane-independent **`PB_TEMPLATE_SUFFIX_TOKS`** rather than `G3_SUFFIX_TOKS`, because `g3_retrieval.h` is included only under the G3 gate — the same gating mistake one level down. **#9:** PB computes its stop reason UNCONDITIONALLY (it now leaves the process) and sends it as a new `uint8_t stop_reason` on the EXISTING `MSG_INFER_STATS` (0x11) — an **internal IPC struct, not a wire format**, so no version bump; PA maps `CAP`/`KV-FULL` → reply **verdict 4** = *answered but cut off*, a new VALUE on the existing 1-byte field (no wire change, no JRPL v3, no 12-place lockstep). `MODEL-ENDED-TURN` is **NOT** truncation — marking it so would cry wolf on nearly every short answer. PA **clears the latch before dispatch** (it is global and the workload lane hits its 50-token cap constantly, so without the clear nearly every control-IN answer would read truncated) and guards on `!served_locally` (a SYSFACTS/DECLINE answer ran no generation). **The text is delivered identically either way — verdict 4 is a marker ON a real answer, not an error state.** This also closes the ANSWER_QUALITY_DESIGN §12 honest-limit *"the stop reason is not observable in production"*: it is now observable ON THE WIRE, where it matters, rather than in a probe-gated log a deployed build never writes. Full record: `phase6/docs/ANSWER_QUALITY_DESIGN.md` §13. **T1c 2026-07-29 — the BOUNDED variant was MEASURED and REJECTED; do not re-run it on a hunch.** `"…first sentence, then add one sentence of detail."` was A/B'd against the shipped string over two otherwise-identical KVM runs (verdict pre-registered, objective sentence counts only). **The benefit was REAL** — all three open questions gained a genuine second sentence (Q4 finally says what the OS *does* about a page fault) — **but all three "in one line" questions ALSO gained one, 3 for 3**, which is `1df800a`'s net-harmful `", then elaborate."` failure reproduced at smaller dose: the instruction beats the question. Verdict **DO NOT SHIP**, source reverted byte-identical. Side-findings: Q7 (`"in three sentences"`) produced exactly 3 in BOTH arms, so neither instruction overrides an explicit sentence count; and one Arm-A answer got SHORTER in bytes while gaining a sentence, so the clause changes answer SHAPE, not just length. The fix a future attempt needs is **conditionality** (elaborate only when the question does not ask for brevity) — a different design, not a reword. Full record: `ANSWER_QUALITY_DESIGN.md` §14.
- **NVMe Partition Setup:** `phase3/scripts/setup_nvme_partition.sh` — creates `JARVIS_DATA` at EXACTLY sector 32768 (hard-asserted via `parted unit s print`; was free-space start → silent model-not-found), default model = Gemma 4 E2B Q4_K_M, copies as `GEMMA2B.GUF` + validates the FAT 8.3 short name == `JARVIS_MODEL_FILE` (`GEMMA2B GUF`), detects+verifies an existing `JARVIS_DATA` instead of recreating (box-safe)
- **x86 Installer (one-script):** `phase3/scripts/install_jarvis_x86.sh` — orchestrates build → boot-USB → NVMe model provision → verify → BIOS/validation checklist; `--build/--usb/--model/--nvme/--repo/--skip-*/--dry-run`; `--target usb` (repartition+format a USB) **and `--target esp` — reversible DUAL-BOOT on the existing internal ESP** (`--esp /dev/nvme0n1p4`: `guard_esp_device` = vfat-ESP-partition check + `ADD-JARVIS` double-confirm, deliberately accepts an NVMe PARTITION but refuses a whole disk; `write_boot_esp` mounts the EXISTING ESP, copies to `EFI/jarvis/` ONLY, sed-rewrites grub.cfg → `/EFI/jarvis/boot/`, `grub-mkstandalone`s `EFI/jarvis/grubx64.efi` — **never** `--removable` / whole-disk / `EFI/BOOT` / `EFI/ubuntu`; additive `efibootmgr` entry with Ubuntu kept `BootOrder[0]`; stale-ESP-root-model cleanup runs FIRST (the box ESP is ~100% full with a 1.79 GB stale copy) behind an explicit `DELETE-STALE-ESP-MODEL` re-type, THEN staging (so the cp's can't ENOSPC-fail; the cp's are guarded to fail loud + unmount on a still-full ESP) — ESP copy only, JARVIS_DATA p2 untouched; rollback printed; **reversible dual-boot — VERIFIED on-box 2026-06-25 (boot_id=5, coherent Gemma 4 E2B, NN=6, err=0; Ubuntu kept `BootOrder[0]`); the boot USB is now RECOVERY-only, not required to boot. F3 fixed (no `set -e` abort when ubuntu is the only prior UEFI entry; regression test T9)**); `disk` = full single-OS on-SSD install that WIPES the whole disk (JARVIS only, no Ubuntu): `guard_disk_device` (whole-disk only; refuses the booted/root + mounted disk + partition paths; WIPE-AND-INSTALL-JARVIS + DESTROY-ALL-DATA triple-confirm) + `write_boot_disk` (wipefs/sgdisk zap → GPT → JARVIS_DATA @ 32768 ~10 GiB + ESP `--removable` BOOTX64.EFI; telemetry-log tail unpartitioned) — **CODE + DRY-RUN ONLY, NEVER run on the dev box** (ADR `docs/decisions/2026-06-25-target-disk-full-ssd-install.md`); forward-structured (`stage_boot_payload(esp_mount, mode)` shared by USB); derives the rootserver image name dynamically (no hardcoded `sel4test-driver-image`); device-safety refuses `/dev/nvme*` + root disk; sourceable pure helpers. **Phase 4 goal #4 (core installer) DONE.** Host test `phase3/scripts/test_install_jarvis_x86.sh` (helpers + full dry-run incl. the esp + disk dry-runs, no-`--esp`/`--disk` errors, partition/root-disk refusals + touches-nothing sentinels, **50 PASS**); both shellcheck-clean (full strictness) + dry-run CI-gated. `reflash_usb.sh` now refuses NVMe/root disk (matching `create_boot_usb.sh`). `BARE_METAL_BOOT_GUIDE.md` corrected (real `sel4test-driver-image` name, 32GB/R9 280X hardware, +NVMe-provisioning + network-telemetry validation sections).
- **Check CI:** `gh run list --limit 1` then `gh run view <id> --log-failed` if failed

### Rules
- Always update CLAUDE.md and week status files after completing work
- COMMIT WEEKLY with week number in message
- Update `phase2/docs/PHASE_2_PROGRESS_TRACKER.md` when completing work
- Test everything: aim for 100% pass rate
- When creating new test files (test_*.c or test_*.py), ALWAYS add a corresponding step to `.github/workflows/ci.yml` with the exact compile+run command. Verify the new CI step compiles locally before committing.
- After every `git push`, check the GitHub Actions workflow status with `gh run list --limit 1` and `gh run view` to verify CI passed. If any step failed, investigate with `gh run view --log-failed` and fix before continuing.
- Phase 2 is C-only on Pi 4 (no Python runtime on seL4)
- **UI–feature parity:** when shipping a user-visible feature, update the Remote Telemetry Console (`phase4/console/`) **in the same change** — add its real live signal to the relevant screen or its auto-populated Capabilities/Features section (never hardcoded). The UI shows only real/live state: a real feature missing from the UI is a gap; UI showing anything without a live source is fiction. Enforced by `phase4/console/test_console_honesty.py`.
- **Frontend (`phase4/console/`) stays correct:** runtime libs (React/Babel/Lucide) are **vendored** under `phase4/console/vendor/` (pinned — no live CDN) so CI is hermetic. Layered tests, all Python (Playwright's **Python** binding — `pip`, no Node): the honesty grep gate + a **key-contract** test (`jarvis_telemetry.h`→`packet_to_record`→`telemetry.js` shape, against one golden fixture) + a **logic** test (`telemetry.js` connState/CRC/stale-watchdog via Playwright `page.clock`) + a real-SSE **e2e smoke** (page boots / no console errors / `simulated==false` / **every live `flags_list` flag renders a Capabilities row**). **All four layers now exist and are CI-gated** (honesty 92 + key-contract/receiver 136 + logic 14 + e2e 34; Playwright installed in CI via `--with-deps --only-shell chromium`, pinned `playwright==1.60.0`). Keep them green; one visual confirm per visual change (the e2e covers the rest).

- Per-assertion PASS counts run higher than file counts (self-reported per-suite tallies, not a measured total). Current host-suite figures re-run 2026-07-25: console honesty **199**, telemetry receiver **275**, console e2e **49**, console logic **25**, `test_jarvis_telemetry.c` **91**.
- **Security:** March 2026 adversarial audit — 26 findings, all resolved (phase2+phase3 C). April 2026 Phase 3c audit — 25 findings (all 5 HIGH + 5 MEDIUM fixed, 7 LOW/INFO accepted). Phase 2's own audit: 6 C findings (4 fixed) + Snyk. SHIELD: host harness (test_shield.c exercises shield.c — keyword + model-assisted scoring); the LIVE QUERY PA↔PB path stays passive — Process B returns ALLOW, shield.c not linked on the query path, so the deployed system does NOT block queries (unchanged, by design). **Phase 6 K/M4 (2026-07-08) closes SEC-039 FOR THE ACTION PATH** — the deployed image now runs the SEPARATE `shield_action.c` ACTION gate LIVE (self-heal PB crash/wedge restarts are SHIELD-scored + JACT-audited; K/M1 proved the mechanism, K/M4 flipped `JARVIS_ACTIONS` default-ON, boot_id=15 supervised on-wire: v7 crc_ok, `TLM_F_ACTIONS` set, restart/fired/blocked honest-0, err=0, NN=6). So the deployed system now has a live, SHIELD-scored, JACT-audited ACTION gate + autonomous self-healing — NOT a query blocker for the passive PA↔PB workload path (never claim "SHIELD blocks queries" / "blocking active"). **Phase 6 6-5 (2026-07-21) additionally closes SEC-039 FOR CONTROL-IN QUERIES** — every inbound control-IN query is scored by the SEPARATE `query_shield.c` gate before PA will route it, and a refusal is JACT-audited by reason-class LABEL only. **Honest scope, non-negotiable:** that gate refuses four **DEFINED ABUSE CLASSES** (key-extraction / bulk-exfil / canned-jailbreak / config-disclose) at a **measured FP of 0/100** on realistic traffic — it is **NOT a general injection detector**; general prompt injection is contained **STRUCTURALLY** (K-b: inbound text can never mint an action; tagged-untrusted so it never contaminates the cache/retrieval stores; the answer is unicast to the provisioned console address only) — **contained, not detected**. The control-IN reply is **SIGNED (HMAC-SHA256), NOT ENCRYPTED**, and third-host non-observation of that unicast reply remains **NOT PROVEN** (it needs a wired capture point).
- **Inference:** 11 GGUF models across 6 model families on JARVIS engine. Gemma 4 E2B (8.40/10 quality — ⚠️ measured RAW-COMPLETION, no chat template; the evidence behind the model choice is weaker than assumed, `phase6/docs/THINKING_MODE_RESEARCH.md` §7) is the single deployed model. Qwen3.5 DeltaNet SSM hybrid working. 3.22 tok/s (1T), 19.79 tok/s (16T) — NATIVE dev engine (Llama 1B), NOT the deployed seL4 build; deployed = Gemma 4 E2B **5.46 tok/s @ NUM_NODES=6**.

### Hardware Setup

**Two-PC setup (April 2026):**
- **Main PC:** Ryzen 5 5600, RTX 2070, 32GB, ASRock B550M-ITX/ac — daily driver
- **JARVIS PC:** Ryzen 7 2700X, R9 280X (display only), 32GB DDR4 (swapped from 16GB), ASUS X470-F, 2TB NVMe, Ubuntu — dedicated, wipeable for bare-metal seL4
- **Pi 4 8GB:** Phase 2 complete, ARM fallback
- CPU-only AI inference on JARVIS PC (no GPU compute). GPU deferred to Phase 4.
- X470-F Gaming NIC: Intel I211-AT — needs igb-family driver (nic_rtl8168.c targets B550M's RTL8111H)
- Phase 2 hardware pivot doc: `phase2/docs/PHASE_2_HARDWARE_PIVOT.md`

### Technology Stack

- **Microkernel:** seL4 (formally verified*) on ARM64 + x86-64 — *\*the x86-64 build runs `KernelFastpath=ON`, outside seL4's verified X64 config ("no fast path"); functional-but-unverified by design (perf > proof) — see `phase4/docs/PHASE_4_GOAL1_INFERENCE_PERF.md` §3*
- **AI Models:** 11 GGUF models across 6 model families — Llama, Gemma 4 (PLE/SWA/KV-sharing), Phi-3 (fused QKV), Mistral (Q8_0), Qwen3 (QK norms), Qwen3.5 (DeltaNet SSM hybrid)
- **Inference:** Quantized zero-copy (Q4_0/Q4_K/Q5_K/Q6_K/Q8_0/BF16 dequant on-the-fly, ~50MB heap). Gated DeltaNet SSM for Qwen3.5 hybrid layers.
- **Build:** TII seL4 build system + CMake/Ninja
- **Cross-compiler:** aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc 13.3.0 (ARM64), gcc 13.3.0 (x86-64)
- **SIMD:** AVX2+FMA for tensor_matmul/rms_norm/qmatmul_vec/qdot/attention (`-mavx2 -mfma`); `#ifdef __AVX2__` fallback to scalar
- **Bootloader:** U-Boot 2026.01 (Pi 4), GRUB2/multiboot (x86 QEMU)
- **Hardware:** Raspberry Pi 4 (BCM2711, 8GB), JARVIS PC (Ryzen 7 2700X, R9 280X display, 32GB, 2TB NVMe, ASUS X470-F, Ubuntu), Main PC (Ryzen 5 5600, RTX 2070, 32GB, ASRock B550M-ITX/ac)
- **QEMU:** KVM-accelerated x86-64, 4GB RAM, CNode 19 (524K slots)
- **Design tooling — Claude Design** (Anthropic Labs, claude.ai/design): a repo-aware AI design tool — it reads the codebase + design files to build a design system, then designs polished pages/prototypes using our real components/tokens, and hands off to Claude Code to land the code. The current Remote Telemetry Console (`phase4/console/`) UI was produced this way. New console screens (e.g. the System screen) are prompt-designed in Claude Design against the repo's design system, then implemented + honesty-gated by Claude Code. Claude Design designs the look; it cannot invent live data — every rendered field must have a real telemetry source.
